Oliver's mTOR Atlas · Researchers

Researchers

903 scientists whose published work is curated in the Atlas, each with a study-by-study timeline and a short account of why their work matters to the mTOR pathway.

Open the sortable researcher ranking →

David M. Sabatini — Identified mTOR (as RAFT1) in 1994. His lab then mapped much of the machinery around it: raptor and rictor at the core of mTORC1 and mTORC2, the Rag–Ragulator system that docks mTORC1 on the lysosome, and the sensors that detect leucine, arginine and SAM · IOCB Prague · Howard Hughes Medical Institute (2008–2021) · Whitehead Institute & MIT (1997–2021) · Born 1968, New York, USA · 57 studies · closest to human: H

Michael N. Hall — Discovered TOR in yeast in 1991 and established it as a central controller of cell growth that responds to nutrients. His lab later showed that TOR works in two distinct complexes, TORC1 and TORC2, only one of which is blocked by rapamycin · Biozentrum, University of Basel, Switzerland · Lasker, Gairdner & Breakthrough Prize laureate · Born 1953, Puerto Rico · 20 studies · closest to human: A

Brendan D. Manning — Was among the groups that in 2002 identified TSC2 as a direct target of Akt (MAN2002), linking insulin and PI3K signalling to mTORC1, and later found TBC1D7 as the third subunit of the TSC complex. His lab has shown how mTORC1 turns growth signals into the synthesis of lipids and nucleotides · Harvard T.H. Chan School of Public Health & Harvard Medical School · postdoc with Lewis Cantley · PhD, Yale · 17 studies · closest to human: A

George Thomas — Purified and cloned S6K1, the kinase that became the classic readout of mTORC1 activity. His lab later showed that mice lacking S6K1 resist diet-induced obesity, and that amino acids signal to mTORC1 through the lipid kinase hVps34 (GUL2008) · Principal investigator, Metabolism and Cancer group, Bellvitge Biomedical Research Institute (IDIBELL), Barcelona · previously Scientific Director, Metabolic Disease Institute, University of Cincinnati · Senior Group Leader, Friedrich Miescher Institute, Basel (1979–2004) · PhD, University of California · 14 studies · closest to human: A

John Blenis — Showed early on that rapamycin blocks S6 kinase, then worked out how mTORC1 drives cell growth through its two main translation targets, S6K1 and 4E-BP1. His lab also found, in parallel with the Sabatini lab, that mTORC1 phosphorylates Grb10, a feedback brake on insulin signalling · Weill Cornell Medicine, New York · Harvard Medical School (1989–2014) · PhD, Michigan State (1983) · 14 studies · closest to human: A

Kun-Liang Guan — Showed how growth-factor and energy signals reach mTORC1 through TSC2 and Rheb, and, in parallel with the Sabatini lab, that the Rag GTPases carry the amino-acid signal. His lab also found that AMPK and mTORC1 control autophagy by phosphorylating the kinase ULK1 at different sites · Westlake University · UC San Diego · University of Michigan · MacArthur Fellow (1998) · Born 1963, Zhejiang, China · 14 studies · closest to human: A

Roberto Zoncu — Showed as a postdoc that mTORC1 senses amino acids from inside the lysosome, through the v-ATPase and Ragulator. His Berkeley lab has since mapped how the Rag GTPases and the folliculin complex switch mTORC1 on, and how lysosomal cholesterol feeds into the same system · Professor, UC Berkeley (Molecular & Cell Biology) · postdoc with David Sabatini · PhD, Yale · neuroscience, University of Pisa · Born in Sardinia, Italy · 14 studies · closest to human: A

Lewis C. Cantley — Co-discovered PI3K, the pathway that feeds into mTOR from growth factors · Professor of Cell Biology, Dana-Farber Cancer Institute & Harvard Medical School · director of the Sandra and Edward Meyer Cancer Center, Weill Cornell Medicine (2012–2022) · co-discovered PI3K (1985) · PhD, Cornell · 12 studies · closest to human: M

Lynne Chantranupong — Showed as a Sabatini-lab graduate student that the Sestrins act through GATOR2 to regulate amino-acid signalling to mTORC1. She then discovered the CASTOR proteins, the cell's arginine sensors for the pathway · Assistant Professor of Biology, Boston University (since 2024) · postdoc and Hanna Gray Fellow, Harvard Medical School (Bernardo Sabatini lab, 2016–2023) · PhD in Biology, MIT (David Sabatini lab, 2016) · BSc, University of Texas at Austin (2010) · 12 studies · closest to human: M

Randy Strong — Leads the San Antonio site of the NIA Interventions Testing Program, which tests each compound in parallel at three sites under one shared protocol. He first-authored the program's reports on resveratrol and other supplements (2012) and on rapamycin combined with acarbose (2022) · Professor of Pharmacology · Co-Director, Nathan Shock Center of Excellence · Barshop Institute for Longevity and Aging Studies, UT Health San Antonio · 12 studies · closest to human: A

Sara C. Kozma — Studies the S6K/mTOR pathway and how it controls cell growth, ribosome production, diabetes and cancer, including mTOR signalling in liver cancer. · Associate Professor, Department of Cancer and Cell Biology, University of Cincinnati (since 2004) · Associate Investigator, Friedrich Miescher Institute, Basel (1987–2003) · 12 studies · closest to human: A

Andrea Ballabio — Discovered that the transcription factor TFEB is a master switch for building lysosomes (SAR2009) and for autophagy. His lab was one of the groups that showed in 2012 that mTORC1 keeps TFEB out of the nucleus from the lysosome surface, and later (2020) that constitutive TFEB activation drives the kidney disease of Birt-Hogg-Dubé syndrome · Professor of Medical Genetics, University of Naples Federico II · founding director of the Telethon Institute of Genetics and Medicine (TIGEM), Naples (1994–2024), now principal investigator there · MD, University of Naples · 11 studies · closest to human: A

Adam B. Salmon — Biologist of aging at UT Health San Antonio whose work in this Atlas spans rapamycin in mice and in common marmosets. He is a co-author on the Interventions Testing Program reports from the San Antonio site and on the marmoset rapamycin studies on blood markers, epigenetic age and the salivary gland · Department of Molecular Medicine · Barshop Institute for Longevity and Aging Studies, UT Health San Antonio · Geriatric Research, Education and Clinical Center, South Texas Veterans Health Care System · 10 studies · closest to human: A

Dudley W. Lamming — Showed as a postdoc that rapamycin's harm to glucose metabolism comes largely from its effect on mTORC2, while the lifespan benefit tracks mTORC1. His Wisconsin lab studies intermittent rapamycin dosing and how restricting dietary protein or single amino acids affects metabolism and ageing · Professor of Medicine, University of Wisconsin–Madison · Co-director, Wisconsin Nathan Shock Center · Postdoc, Whitehead Institute/MIT (Sabatini lab) · 9 studies · closest to human: H

John M. Asara — Mass-spectrometry specialist whose core facility supplied metabolomics and proteomics for several mTOR studies, including measurements of purine, pyrimidine and SAM metabolism downstream of mTORC1. · Director, Mass Spectrometry Core, Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center (founded 2004), and Associate Professor of Medicine, Harvard Medical School · postdoc in proteomics, Harvard University, then biotechnology industry · PhD in Analytical Chemistry, Michigan State University (1999) · 9 studies · closest to human: A

Kuang Shen — Solved, as a Sabatini-lab postdoc, cryo-EM structures of GATOR1 and of the FLCN–FNIP2–Rag–Ragulator complex, two switches that set mTORC1 activity. His UMass Chan lab studies the structural basis of nutrient sensing · Associate Professor, Program in Molecular Medicine, UMass Chan Medical School · PhD in Chemistry, California Institute of Technology · postdoc, David Sabatini lab, Whitehead Institute/MIT · 8 studies · closest to human: M

Liron Bar-Peled — Showed as a Sabatini-lab graduate student that Ragulator switches the Rag GTPases on, acting as their GEF. He also discovered GATOR1 and GATOR2; GATOR1 switches the Rags off and its genes are mutated in some cancers · Rullo Family Endowed Chair in Cancer Research, Massachusetts General Hospital & Harvard Medical School · PhD, Sabatini lab, Whitehead Institute/MIT · 8 studies · closest to human: M

Matt Kaeberlein — Extended TOR/aging research from yeast to mice to pet dogs · CEO, Optispan · co-founder & co-director, Dog Aging Project · co-founder, Dog Aging Institute · Affiliate Professor, University of Washington (previously Professor of Pathology) · PhD, MIT (2002) · 8 studies · closest to human: A

Rachel L. Wolfson — Identified Sestrin2 as the direct leucine sensor of the mTORC1 pathway as a Sabatini-lab MD-PhD student. She also co-discovered KICSTOR, the complex that recruits GATOR1 to the lysosome · Assistant Professor of Cell Biology, Harvard Medical School · postdoc, Department of Neurobiology, Harvard Medical School · clinical training, Massachusetts General Hospital · MD, Harvard Medical School; PhD, MIT (Sabatini lab) · 8 studies · closest to human: A

Robert A. Saxton — Solved, as a Sabatini-lab graduate student, the structures of the leucine sensor Sestrin2 and the arginine sensor CASTOR1, showing how each one grips its amino acid. He also co-wrote a widely cited 2017 review of mTOR signalling with David Sabatini · Assistant Professor of Immunology and Molecular Medicine and of Chemistry, UC Berkeley (Molecular & Cell Biology) · postdoc, Stanford University (K. Christopher Garcia lab) · PhD, Sabatini lab, Whitehead Institute/MIT · 8 studies · closest to human: M

Arlan Richardson — Studies how rapamycin slows ageing in mice, including the broad changes it causes in the liver, and co-authored early studies showing that rapamycin protects memory in Alzheimer's-model mice · Reynolds Chair in Aging Research, University of Oklahoma Health Sciences Center · Director, Oklahoma Nathan Shock Center of Excellence in the Basic Biology of Aging (2015–2022) · PhD, Oklahoma State University · 7 studies · closest to human: A

Carson C. Thoreen — Used Torin1, one of the first ATP-competitive mTOR inhibitors, to reveal functions of mTORC1 that rapamycin cannot block. He then showed that mTORC1 controls translation of mRNAs for the protein-making machinery mainly through the 4E-BP proteins · Associate Professor of Cellular & Molecular Physiology, Yale School of Medicine · PhD, MIT (2008) · trained in the Sabatini lab, Whitehead Institute · 7 studies · closest to human: M

Constantinos Demetriades — Showed as a postdoc in Aurelio Teleman's lab that starvation and other stresses pull TSC2 to the lysosome to switch mTORC1 off. His group, at the Max Planck Institute for Biology of Ageing in Cologne from 2017 and at ERIBA in Groningen since 2025, studies how cells sense nutrients and stress through mTORC1 and how this changes with age · Associate Professor, ERIBA, University Medical Center Groningen (since 2025) · Max Planck Research Group Leader, MPI for Biology of Ageing, Cologne (2017–2025) · postdoc, DKFZ Heidelberg (Aurelio Teleman lab, 2010–2016) · PhD, BSRC 'Alexander Fleming' & Aristotle Univ. (George Mosialos lab, 2003–2009) · BSc, Aristotle Univ. of Thessaloniki (2003) · 7 studies · closest to human: M

David A. Guertin — Built the mouse genetics that defined mTORC2's biological roles · Professor of Molecular Medicine, UMass Chan Medical School · postdoc, Whitehead Institute/MIT (Sabatini lab) · PhD, UMass Chan Medical School · 7 studies · closest to human: A

David E. Harrison — Led the Jackson Laboratory site of the NIA Interventions Testing Program. His 2009 Nature paper showed that rapamycin extends the lifespan of genetically diverse mice even when feeding starts at 20 months of age, which the authors described as the first pharmacological extension of lifespan in both sexes of a mammal · Professor Emeritus, The Jackson Laboratory, Bar Harbor, Maine · PhD, Stanford · 7 studies · closest to human: A

James H. Hurley — Structural biologist who uses cryo-EM to show how protein machines assembled on membranes control autophagy and nutrient signalling. His lab solved structures of the Rag–Ragulator complex and of the whole mTORC1–TFEB–Rag–Ragulator assembly on the lysosome · Professor of Molecular & Cell Biology and Kirsch Springer Chair in Biological Sciences, UC Berkeley (since 2013) · Faculty Scientist, Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory (since 2014) · Chief, Section on Structural Biology & Cell Signaling, NIDDK/NIH (1998–2013) · Senior Investigator, NIDDK/NIH (1997–2013) · Tenure-track Investigator, NIH (1992–1997) · Postdoctoral Fellow, University of Oregon (1990–1992) · PhD in Biophysics, UCSF (1990) · BA in Physics, San Francisco State University (1984) · Member, US National Academy of Sciences (2020) · Hans Neurath Award, Protein Society (2014) · 7 studies · closest to human: M

Ken Inoki — Showed as a postdoc in Kun-Liang Guan's lab that Akt switches off TSC2, that TSC2 is the off-switch (GAP) for the small GTPase Rheb, and that AMPK and GSK3 feed energy and Wnt signals into the same brake. His Michigan lab studies mTOR signalling in metabolism and kidney disease · Roger C. Wiggins Collegiate Professor of the Life Sciences · Research Associate Professor, Life Sciences Institute · Professor of Internal Medicine (Nephrology) and of Molecular & Integrative Physiology, University of Michigan Medical School · MD, PhD · 7 studies · closest to human: M

Richard A. Miller — Leads the University of Michigan site of the NIA Interventions Testing Program. His papers showed that rapamycin extends mouse lifespan when started in middle age, and that the size of the benefit depends on dose and differs between males and females · Professor of Pathology, University of Michigan · Co-Director, Paul F. Glenn Center for Biology of Aging Research, University of Michigan · MD, PhD, Yale · 7 studies · closest to human: A

Seong A. Kang — Contributed to the Sabatini-lab studies that identified PRAS40 and DEPTOR, two of the cell's own mTOR inhibitors, and to the first characterisation of the mTOR inhibitor Torin1 · Whitehead Institute for Biomedical Research, MIT · contributed to the Sabatini lab's PRAS40 and DEPTOR papers (2007–2009) · 7 studies · closest to human: M

David J. Kwiatkowski — Geneticist and physician who studies tuberous sclerosis complex (TSC) and the TSC1/TSC2 genes that restrain mTORC1. · Brigham and Women's Hospital · Senior Physician; Professor of Medicine, Harvard Medical School · 6 studies · closest to human: A

Joseph Avruch — Showed in 1998 that amino acids, not only insulin, are needed for mTOR to activate S6K1 and 4E-BP1. His Massachusetts General Hospital group, with Kazuyoshi Yonezawa's lab in Kobe, then co-discovered raptor in 2002 and found that Rheb binds mTOR directly · Professor of Medicine, Harvard Medical School · Chief, Diabetes Unit, Massachusetts General Hospital (since 1979) · MD, Washington University in St. Louis · 6 studies · closest to human: M

Nahum Sonenberg — Co-discovered the mRNA cap-binding protein eIF4E and the 4E-BP proteins that restrain it, and showed how mTOR releases this brake to control protein synthesis · Distinguished James McGill Professor of Biochemistry and Gilman Cheney Chair, McGill University; member, Rosalind and Morris Goodman Cancer Institute · joined McGill University (1979) · postdoc, Roche Institute of Molecular Biology (Aaron Shatkin lab) · PhD, Weizmann Institute of Science (1976) · BSc & MSc in microbiology and immunology, Tel Aviv University · 6 studies · closest to human: A

Steven P. Gygi — Mass-spectrometry scientist whose Harvard lab builds large-scale proteomics methods, including the BioPlex human protein-interaction map, and contributed proteomics to the discoveries of the CASTOR and SAMTOR nutrient sensors · Professor of Cell Biology, Harvard Medical School; faculty director, Taplin Biological Mass Spectrometry Facility and the Thermo Fisher Center for Multiplexed Proteomics · own lab, Harvard Medical School (since 2000) · postdoc, University of Washington (Ruedi Aebersold lab, from 1996) · PhD in Pharmacology and Toxicology (mass spectrometry), University of Utah · 6 studies · closest to human: M

Timothy R. Peterson — Discovered DEPTOR, an inhibitor that binds mTOR in both complexes and is overexpressed in some multiple myelomas. He also showed that mTORC1 controls fat synthesis by regulating where the enzyme lipin 1 sits in the cell, and through it the SREBP pathway · CEO, BIOIO · previously ran a lab at Washington University School of Medicine in St. Louis · PhD, David Sabatini lab, Whitehead Institute/MIT · 6 studies · closest to human: M

Aurelio A. Teleman — Uses fruit-fly genetics and human cells to study how growth signalling through TOR controls cell and body size. His lab showed that starvation and other stresses move TSC2 to the lysosome to switch mTORC1 off · Division Head, DKFZ (since 2012) · Junior Group Leader, DKFZ (2007) · McKinsey & Co. (2001–2002) · PhD, EMBL & Imperial College London (Stephen M. Cohen, 2004) · AB Biochemistry, Harvard (Richard Losick, 1998) · EMBO Member (2020) · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Clinton Astle — Long-time research associate of David Harrison at The Jackson Laboratory, with work on mouse blood stem cells and aging since the 1970s, and a co-author of the Interventions Testing Program papers that showed rapamycin extends mouse lifespan · The Jackson Laboratory, Bar Harbor, Maine (papers with David E. Harrison, 1976–2019) · 5 studies · closest to human: A

David Lebwohl — Medical oncologist and drug developer who led the clinical development of everolimus (Afinitor) at Novartis through approvals in several cancers and tuberous sclerosis, and later served as Chief Medical Officer of the gene-editing company Intellia Therapeutics · Executive Vice President and Chief Medical Officer, Intellia Therapeutics (2020–2026) · Chief Medical Officer, Semma Therapeutics (2018–2019) · Senior Vice President and Global Program Head, Novartis (everolimus, CAR-T and other programs) · Oncology Clinical Development, Bristol-Myers Squibb · MD, Yale University School of Medicine · AB in Biochemical Sciences, Harvard College · 5 studies · closest to human: H

David N. Franz — Led the EXIST-1 trial showing that the mTOR inhibitor everolimus shrinks the brain tumours (SEGAs) of tuberous sclerosis, and helped run the first sirolimus trial for its kidney tumours · Professor of Pediatrics, Univ. of Cincinnati · Founding Director, TSC Clinic, Cincinnati Children's · MD, pediatric neurologist · 5 studies · closest to human: H

Do-Hyung Kim — Co-discovered raptor, the defining partner of mTOR in mTORC1, and then mLST8 (GbetaL) as a Sabatini-lab postdoc. His Minnesota lab identified PRAS40 as an insulin-regulated link to mTORC1 and studies how mTORC1 controls autophagy · Professor, Metabolic & Systems Biology, University of Minnesota · Postdoc, Whitehead Institute/MIT (Sabatini lab) · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Dos D. Sarbassov — Discovered rictor as a Sabatini-lab postdoc, in parallel with Michael Hall's lab, defining mTOR Complex 2, and showed that this complex is the long-sought kinase that phosphorylates Akt on serine 473. He also found that prolonged rapamycin treatment can block mTORC2 assembly · Professor of Biology, Nazarbayev University, Kazakhstan · formerly Dept. of Molecular & Cellular Oncology, MD Anderson Cancer Center · Postdoc, Whitehead Institute/MIT (Sabatini lab) · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Ernst Hafen — Developmental geneticist who used Drosophila to work out how insulin and TOR signalling control cell and body size, including the role of Rheb. · Professor of Developmental Genetics, Institute of Molecular Systems Biology, ETH Zurich (since 2006) · Professor, University of Zurich (1997–2005) · Postdoc with Gerald Rubin, UC Berkeley (1984–1986) · 5 studies · closest to human: A

Estela Jacinto — Co-discovered mammalian mTORC2 as a postdoc in Michael Hall's lab, showing that it controls the actin cytoskeleton and is insensitive to rapamycin. Her Rutgers lab, with Bing Su's group, was among those that found SIN1 holds mTORC2 together and studies how both mTOR complexes control metabolism · Professor of Biochemistry & Molecular Biology, Rutgers Robert Wood Johnson Medical School · postdoc, Biozentrum, University of Basel (Michael Hall lab) · PhD, UC San Diego · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Gregory A. Wyant — Showed that the lysosomal protein SLC38A9 exports leucine and other essential amino acids out of the lysosome, letting cells grow on proteins they have taken up and broken down there · Assistant Professor, MGH/Harvard Medical School · postdoc, Dana-Farber (William Kaelin lab) · PhD, MIT/Whitehead (Sabatini lab) · BS, Northeastern Univ. · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Hediye Erdjument-Bromage — Ran the mass-spectrometry analyses in Paul Tempst's lab at Memorial Sloan Kettering that identified the unknown proteins in the raptor, mLST8 and rictor purifications, and contributed the same approach to work on how ERK inactivates TSC2 · Proteomics & mass spectrometry, NYU Langone Health Mass Spectrometry Core for Neuroscience · previously Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Issam Ben-Sahra — Showed as a postdoc in Brendan Manning's lab that mTORC1 drives both pyrimidine and purine synthesis, through two different mechanisms. His Northwestern lab has extended this to bicarbonate import, SAM synthesis and ERK control of purine production · Associate Professor, Northwestern Feinberg (since 2023) · postdoc, Harvard T.H. Chan School (Brendan D. Manning lab) · PhD, Université de Nice Sophia Antipolis (now Université Côte d'Azur), France (Frédéric Bost lab, 2010) · 5 studies · closest to human: M

José Baselga — Breast cancer oncologist who led the BOLERO-2 trial showing that the mTOR inhibitor everolimus added to hormone therapy lengthens progression-free survival · Deceased (1959–2021) · Executive Vice President, Oncology R&D, AstraZeneca (2019–2021) · Physician-in-Chief, Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center · President, AACR (2015–2016) · Massachusetts General Hospital Cancer Center (affiliation on BAS2012, 2011) · 5 studies · closest to human: H

Kazuyoshi Yonezawa — Led the Kobe University group that co-discovered raptor in 2002, in parallel with the Sabatini lab. His team then showed that raptor binds mTOR's substrates through their TOS motif and that PRAS40 is itself an mTORC1 substrate · Biosignal Research Center, Kobe University, Japan · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Monther Abu-Remaileh — Worked out how to isolate lysosomes fast enough to measure what is inside them, and found that mTOR controls what gets out · Assistant Professor of Chemical Engineering and of Genetics, Stanford University · postdoctoral research, Whitehead Institute and MIT, in David Sabatini's laboratory (to 2019) · PhD in Molecular Genetics, The Hebrew University of Jerusalem (2014) · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Nancy L. Nadon — Scientist and programme official at the US National Institute on Aging who oversaw research resources for aging biology and helped run the Interventions Testing Program that found rapamycin extends mouse lifespan · Division of Aging Biology, National Institute on Aging (at the time of the studies) · Chief, Biological Resources Branch (listed 2013) · earlier research at NINDS, NIH, on myelin genetics (1990) · 5 studies · closest to human: A

Paul Tempst — Developed mass-spectrometry methods for identifying proteins from tiny samples, and led the Memorial Sloan Kettering proteomics laboratory that identified the unknown partners of mTOR in the raptor, mLST8 and rictor purifications · Molecular Biology Program, Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center · mass-spectrometry protein identification · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Reuben J. Shaw — Showed, in parallel with two other groups, that the tumour suppressor LKB1 directly activates AMPK, and that AMPK brakes mTORC1 by phosphorylating raptor. His Salk lab also linked AMPK to autophagy through ULK1 and helped explain how metformin acts in the liver · Professor, Salk Institute for Biological Studies · postdoc with Lewis Cantley, Harvard Medical School · PhD, MIT (advisor: Tyler Jacks) · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Robbie Loewith — Showed, as first author in Michael Hall's lab, that yeast TOR exists as two distinct complexes, the basis of the TORC1/TORC2 framework · Full Professor, Univ. of Geneva (faculty since 2005, full professor since 2015) · postdoc, Univ. of Basel (Michael N. Hall) · PhD, Univ. of Calgary (Dallan Young) · BSc, Univ. of Guelph · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Siraj M. Ali — Worked in the Sabatini lab on the papers that discovered raptor, mLST8 and rictor, and on the finding that the rictor-mTOR complex phosphorylates Akt. He later moved into cancer genomics and clinical diagnostics · Head of Translational Medicine, Lunit · previously Foundation Medicine and EQRx · graduate research, Sabatini lab, Whitehead Institute/MIT (co-author on the raptor, mLST8 and rictor papers) · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Sonia M. Scaria — Contributed, as an MIT undergraduate in the Sabatini lab, to the discovery of CASTOR1 as the arginine sensor and SAMTOR as the SAM sensor of the mTORC1 pathway · UCSF medical student, continuing hearing-loss genetics research · MD/PhD, UCSF MSTP — PhD in Tward lab, UCSF Otolaryngology (2019–2022, tympanic-membrane regeneration) · BS Biological Engineering, MIT (2017; undergrad researcher, Sabatini lab) · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Tim Wang — Co-developed genome-wide CRISPR screening in human cells (reported in parallel by Feng Zhang's lab) as a graduate student with David Sabatini and Eric Lander, and contributed to studies showing that the 4E-BPs mediate mTORC1's control of translation and that CASTOR1 senses arginine · Senior Scientist, Allen Institute for Neural Dynamics · postdoc (Helen Hay Whitney Fellow), Janelia Research Campus (Karel Svoboda lab) · PhD, MIT (Sabatini & Lander labs) · BS Bioengineering, UC Berkeley · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Viktor I. Korolchuk — Showed that where lysosomes sit inside the cell tunes mTORC1 activity to nutrient supply. His Newcastle lab found that senescent cells can no longer switch mTORC1 off when nutrients or growth factors are withdrawn, and studies how autophagy and mitophagy decline with age · Professor of Cell Biology, Biosciences Institute, Newcastle University (since 2024) · Reader, Newcastle University (2017–2024) · Lecturer, Institute of Ageing and Health, Newcastle University (2011–2017) · postdoc, University of Cambridge (2003–2011, David Rubinsztein lab) · postdoc, University of Bristol (2000–2003) · PhD in Biochemistry, Institute of Biochemistry, National Academy of Sciences of Ukraine, Kyiv (2000) · 5 studies · closest to human: A

Yasemin Sancak — Showed as a Sabatini-lab graduate student, in parallel with Kun-Liang Guan's lab, that the Rag GTPases relay amino-acid signals to mTORC1, and that Ragulator anchors them to the lysosome where mTORC1 is switched on. She also identified PRAS40 as an insulin-regulated inhibitor of mTORC1 · Associate Professor of Pharmacology, University of Washington · Pew Biomedical Scholar · postdoc, Vamsi Mootha lab, Massachusetts General Hospital & Broad Institute · PhD, Sabatini lab, Whitehead Institute/MIT · 5 studies · closest to human: M

Alessandra Esposito — Lysosomal signalling and TFEB regulation · Telethon Institute of Genetics and Medicine (TIGEM), Naples · Scuola Superiore Meridionale (SSM), Genomics and Experimental Medicine programme · 4 studies · closest to human: M

Alexander J. Valvezan — Showed as a Manning-lab postdoc that mTORC1 matches nucleotide production to demand, creating a weak point that can be targeted in tumours with TSC loss. His Rutgers lab found that mTORC1 activity rises and falls through the cell cycle and helps drive entry into mitosis · Assistant Professor of Pharmacology, Rutgers Robert Wood Johnson Medical School · postdoc, Harvard T.H. Chan School of Public Health (Brendan Manning lab) · PhD, University of Pennsylvania · 4 studies · closest to human: M

Benoit Viollet — Showed that metformin still lowers liver glucose production without AMPK or LKB1, pointing instead to a drop in cellular energy charge · Directeur de Recherche (INSERM), Institut Cochin · shifted to AMPK biology late 1990s · trained in Axel Kahn's lab, Institut Cochin de Génétique Moléculaire · 4 studies · closest to human: A

Bing Su — Immunologist who studies how mTOR and MAPK signaling shape immune regulation and vascular function, and senior author of the study showing that SIN1 is a core part of mTORC2. · Director, Shanghai Institute of Immunology, Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine · Adjunct Professor, Yale School of Medicine · Joined SJTU 2012 · PhD, Yale (1991) · 4 studies · closest to human: M

Brian K. Kennedy — Showed with Matt Kaeberlein that reducing TOR or Sch9 signalling extends yeast lifespan in the same way as dietary restriction, and has since championed mTOR as a target for slowing human ageing · Distinguished Professor of Biochemistry & Physiology, National University of Singapore (since 2017) · President & CEO, Buck Institute for Research on Aging (2010–2016) · Assistant/Associate Professor, University of Washington (with Matt Kaeberlein) · postdoc, Massachusetts General Hospital Cancer Center · PhD, MIT (Leonard Guarente lab) · 4 studies · closest to human: A

Christian C. Dibble — Identified TBC1D7 as the third subunit of the TSC complex that controls mTORC1, as a Manning-lab graduate student. His lab at Beth Israel Deaconess studies how PI3K signalling and nutrient supply are integrated to control mTORC1 and cell metabolism · Assistant Professor of Pathology, Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center & Harvard Medical School · PhD, Brendan Manning lab, Harvard T.H. Chan School of Public Health · postdoc, Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center (Lewis Cantley lab) · 4 studies · closest to human: M

Davide Ruggero — Showed that oncogenic mTOR signalling reprograms which mRNAs are translated, and that tumours become addicted to this through the 4E-BP–eIF4E axis. His UCSF lab found that mTOR-driven translation of a specific set of mRNAs promotes prostate cancer invasion and metastasis · Professor of Urology & Cellular and Molecular Pharmacology, UCSF Helen Diller Family Comprehensive Cancer Center · PhD, University of Rome La Sapienza · 4 studies · closest to human: A

Gennaro Napolitano — mTORC1-TFEB lysosomal signalling · Telethon Institute of Genetics and Medicine (TIGEM), Naples · Medical Genetics Unit, Federico II University, Naples · 4 studies · closest to human: M

Gerta Hoxhaj — Studies how growth signalling through PI3K, AKT and mTOR rewires cell metabolism; as a Manning-lab postdoc she co-authored work on how metformin inhibits mTORC1 in the liver · Associate Professor, UT Southwestern (faculty since 2019) · postdoc, Brendan Manning lab, Harvard T.H. Chan School (2013–2019) · PhD, MRC Protein Phosphorylation Unit, Univ. of Dundee (2013) · BSc, Boğaziçi University, Istanbul · 4 studies · closest to human: R

Jonathan D. Powell — Showed that T cells lacking mTOR become regulatory T cells instead of effector cells, and that mTORC1 and mTORC2 each steer a different type of helper T cell · Fellow, Calico Life Sciences · 20 years at Johns Hopkins University (Bloomberg-Kimmel Institute for Cancer Immunotherapy) · MD, PhD, Emory University School of Medicine · 4 studies · closest to human: A

Jose M. Orozco — Researcher trained in David Sabatini's laboratory at the Whitehead Institute and MIT who showed that mTORC1 senses glucose through the glycolysis metabolite DHAP · Harvard/MIT MD-PhD Program; PhD student in the Sabatini laboratory, Whitehead Institute and MIT Department of Biology (papers 2014–2021; at the time of the studies) · undergraduate research in Erin Cram's lab, Northeastern University · 4 studies · closest to human: M

Kacper B. Rogala — Solved the near-atomic cryo-EM structure that shows how mTORC1 physically docks at the lysosome by reading the Rag GTPases · Assistant Professor, Stanford (Structural Biology & Chemical & Systems Biology) · postdoc, MIT/Whitehead (Sabatini lab) · DPhil, Univ. of Oxford · MRes, UCL · BSc, Univ. of Edinburgh · 4 studies · closest to human: M

Yonghao Yu — Used phosphoproteomics to show that mTORC1 phosphorylates and stabilises Grb10, which then acts as a feedback brake on insulin and IGF-1 signalling · Professor, Columbia University Irving Medical Center · Associate Professor, UT Southwestern · postdoc, Harvard Medical School (Steven Gygi & John Blenis) · PhD Chemistry, UC Berkeley (Julie Leary) · BS Chemistry, Fudan Univ. · 1 study · closest to human: M

Yongwon Choi — Immunologist who studies TRAF6 and RANKL (TRANCE) signalling at the junction of the immune and skeletal systems; senior author of the study linking TRAF6 and fatty acid oxidation to CD8 T-cell memory · Leonard Jarett Professor of Pathology and Laboratory Medicine, Perelman School of Medicine, University of Pennsylvania · research fellow with John Kappler and Philippa Marrack, Howard Hughes Medical Institute, National Jewish Center for Immunology and Respiratory Medicine, Denver (1988–1992) · PhD in Biochemistry, University of Illinois College of Medicine, Chicago (1988) · BSc, Seoul National University (1984) · 1 study · closest to human: A

Yoshiaki Kamada — Showed as first author that TOR keeps autophagy switched off in yeast by controlling the Atg1 (Apg1) kinase complex, and has studied TOR's control of autophagy ever since · Assistant Professor, National Institute for Basic Biology, National Institutes of Natural Sciences, Okazaki, Japan · ORCID 0000-0001-7395-660X · 1 study · closest to human: M

Yoshinori Ohsumi — Found the genes that make autophagy work, and with them the link from TOR to the autophagy machinery · Honorary Professor, Cell Biology Center, Institute of Integrated Research, Institute of Science Tokyo · Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine 2016, “for his discoveries of mechanisms for autophagy” · PhD, University of Tokyo (1974) · postdoctoral research, Rockefeller University · born 1945 · 1 study · closest to human: M

Young Chul Kim — Cell biologist who studied mTOR and autophagy in Kun-Liang Guan's lab at UC San Diego and later moved to kidney research in Volker Vallon's group · Division of Nephrology and Hypertension, UC San Diego, and VA San Diego Healthcare System (papers with Volker Vallon, 2024–2026) · Department of Pharmacology and Moores Cancer Center, UC San Diego (Kun-Liang Guan lab, papers 2013–2015) · 1 study · closest to human: M

Young-Mi Kim — mTOR researcher in Do-Hyung Kim's University of Minnesota lab who found SH3BP4 as a brake on the amino acid sensing Rag GTPases and showed that mTORC1 phosphorylates UVRAG to slow autophagy · Department of Biochemistry, Molecular Biology and Biophysics, University of Minnesota (papers with Do-Hyung Kim, 2007–2016) · 1 study · closest to human: M

Yu Liu — Cancer biologist working on how tumour-suppressor loss and epigenetic state decide whether a cell stays differentiated · Professor, National Key Laboratory of Biotherapy, West China Hospital, Sichuan University (since 2015) · Postdoctoral Fellow, Cold Spring Harbor Laboratory & Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center (2010–2015) · Postdoctoral Fellow, University of Michigan (2008–2010) · PhD, Albert Einstein College of Medicine (2008) · MS, Beijing Normal University (2000) · BS, Beijing Normal University (1997) · 1 study · closest to human: A

Knight — Neuroscientist whose lab studies the brain mechanisms behind appetite, thirst and body-temperature preference; he is a co-author of the 2009 study that introduced the TORKinibs PP242 and PP30. · Professor, Department of Physiology, UCSF · Investigator, Howard Hughes Medical Institute (named 2018) · Weill Institute for Neurosciences · 1 study · closest to human: M

Yilmaz — Pathologist and stem-cell biologist who studies how diet and nutrient sensing control intestinal stem cells; as a postdoc with David Sabatini he showed that calorie restriction acts on the stem-cell niche through mTORC1 in Paneth cells · Associate Professor of Biology (tenured 2022), Koch Institute for Integrative Cancer Research, MIT · gastrointestinal pathologist, Massachusetts General Hospital and Harvard Medical School · own lab since 2014 · visiting postdoctoral fellow with David Sabatini, Whitehead Institute · thesis work with Sean Morrison, University of Michigan Medical School · 1 study · closest to human: M

Oliver's mTOR Atlas – an evidence-graded database of the mTOR pathway. Every entry traces to a primary paper and is labelled by the kind of study behind it. Curated by Oliver Barton, Prague.
